October 6 native Vulkan sharp MODEL reflection-history follow-up (Windows
pinned-owner checks, NOT calibrated application integration, Linux/Deck,
history-resolver reuse, FPS or final 3DS acceptance):

The optional producer emits the canonical 52-byte/pixel current RGBA, previous
reflected-feature motion, explicit current/accepted primitive identities and
current secondary barycentric/depth witness. Previous geometry and the optional
accepted-ID mapping remain in the submitted resident allocation. Motion follows
the secondary feature reflected through the actual previous finite mirror, not
the primary receiver's motion. Missing mapping emits invalid old identities;
invalid W/NaN/degenerate/outside-face/clip/screen correspondence emits zero motion.
Sky escapes have current colour but no finite-hit motion/identity/witness.

This is sharp, native linear/sRGB MODEL-only history with no secondary specular
transport. Separated ground/liquid, compact rough lobes and ordered model/scene
paths explicitly decline rather than aliasing their different contracts. The
new history shader/pipeline is created on demand, with a 464-byte UBO; ordinary
reflection/shadow remain 416/352 bytes and their generated SPIR-V headers are
byte-identical to the preceding lifetime checkpoint. No new descriptors or
source readbacks are introduced. History OFF retains the existing RGBA ABI and
reports any retained larger image capacity.

Coverage, identities and validity are exact. RGB error is at most one byte,

current primary depth within max(.002, abs(expected)*2e-5). History ON/OFF
current RGBA is byte-identical. Twenty-six malformed/unsupported requests per
GPU clear borrowed output and recover every record exactly; four nonblocking
release/reinitialize cycles also preserve all records.

October 6 native Vulkan lifetime follow-up (Windows pinned-owner checks,
NOT calibrated application integration, Linux/Deck, device-loss, FPS or final
3DS acceptance):

`native_work_complete()` queries completion without waiting/readback.
`try_release_device()` clears borrowed outputs but retains all resources until
every producer slot is complete. Explicit blocking cleanup and slot reuse
respect wait failures, rather than freeing GPU-visible storage on an error.
All slots are certified before any teardown. A consumed submission returning
no fence obtains an empty ordered recovery marker; it is not cancelled or
replayed. If completion cannot be established during destruction, the owner is
deliberately retained rather than freeing live resources. The borrowed SDL
device must outlive pending cleanup/retries; this is not a device-loss policy
accepted on a platform. External SDL consumers remain caller-owned.

Initialization and lazy reflection initialization now have explicit readiness
and rollback. Buffer allocation/bind failures destroy their local buffer/memory;
failed partial variant pipeline creation cannot satisfy a cached-ready check.
`working_image_bytes()` reports retained SDL shadow/reflection capacities,
including the larger full-water allocation after a smaller request, not VRAM,
source payloads, acceleration structures, scratch or driver allocations.

The dedicated diagnostic passes on NVIDIA RTX 5070 Ti Laptop and Intel Graphics.

reflection descriptor-pool initialization, buffer allocation/bind, partial
variant pipeline and post-dispatch recording failures. A real Vulkan timeline
gate keeps three producer slots queued and incomplete while checks verify no
poll/try-release wait, no native destruction and retained 86,016 image bytes.
Failed blocking waits/slot reuse and fence-query errors also retain resources.
After signalling, all native buffer/memory/AS/pipeline/descriptor handles retire.
Eight consumed-submit cases per GPU hide one/two real submitted fences over
both producers and repeated cycles. Recovery markers do not replay a native
dispatch or cancel consumed commands; valid retries exactly restore all 3,072
baseline pixels and release tracked handles/image capacities. No shader or

samples and encoded cameras without changing any optical equation or
finished-image tolerance. Water/lava and the final lossless guard remain
unaccepted.
The isolated capture proves both samples' current and accepted native ray

before SDK packing, and a fresh rays-OFF replay reproduces that difference.

The exact original actor texture remains reproducible using the dedicated
`--dlss-ray-liquid-primary-edge-only` switch and its UNCHANGED strict mean;

fixture now separates that primary discontinuity from optical transport
using a continuous but still nonconstant, moving, finite textured actor.
Reflected high-frequency world scenery remains unchanged. New independent
per-eye/sample gates require positive FINITE incident reuse, reconstruct
native RGB in binary64 from recorded incoming/current base/response within

No optical coverage pass is inferred. Water/lava, all four
formats and final lossless teardown remain unaccepted. The retained
original primary-edge CLI independently reproduced the strict 120/115
failure, with its original texture and native/rays-OFF evidence intact.

equations, installed players or packaged native player changed.

October 5 audio compatibility progress: a separate asset-free CSND stereo check
has been added to the native frontend diagnostic (Y; NDSP remains X). It owns
immutable one-shot PCM on two actually granted channels, starts both sides in
one command batch and waits for actual channel status, not an estimated DMA
cursor. NDSP and CSND share a single process output lease. Acknowledgment polls
yield and have a 100 ms deadline; failed/unacknowledged lists retire and poison
the service owner before reuse. Service exit precedes linear-memory release.
The diagnostic does not import the reference's looping ring, frame insertion,
dropped packets or SPC worker scheduling. The game still uses NDSP and its
unchanged two accurate SPC stems. Native CSND streaming/clock matching and
Home/sleep integration are not implemented or accepted by this check.

October 2 hit-selection follow-up: preceding PC `2628AD56...` separates rejected
ray-hit experiments from ordinary shader PSOs. A broad tie resolver passes
the sampled Intel repeat but fails the one-ULP regression; a duplicate-only
resolver still differs in 22/64 native outputs. Precise depth refinement
passes 88 focused samples on each NVIDIA backend but fails Intel's synthetic
and live checks. It remains OFF; this is not a finished reflection fix.
The ordinary current PC passes five exact full-SBS water captures on each
NVIDIA backend, two CPU suites and three ordinary actual-adapter ray component
suites. Component logs and exact hashes/scopes are
in RENDERER-PERFORMANCE-OCT1.md. No ray-hit timing claim is made after failed
correctness checks. Release/settings are unchanged; the full goal remains open.

October 2 ray diagnostic follow-up: current `7A4F0366...` records matching CPU
and resident input fingerprints but varying native Intel ray output, before
SDL import/composition. Raw first-pair positions/materials also match. Primary
hit IDs vary on overlapping faces and some normal-output hashes vary; the
primary-distance repeat is exact. The canonical-normal experiment does not
close the water gate and stays OFF. Quiet timing results below belong to the
preceding CB42012F executable. Release/settings remain unchanged; exact scope,
failed controls and intermediate hashes are in RENDERER-PERFORMANCE-OCT1.md.
The unchanged default passes five exact reflected-water SBS captures on each
NVIDIA backend, three actual-adapter ray suites and two selected CPU suites;
the live Intel gate remains open.

October 2 arithmetic follow-up: current diagnostic `CB42012F...` uses exact
fixed-limb multiplication instead of indexed/per-bit loops, without changing
precision or shader ABI. Host and three-adapter GPU bit checks, independent
stereo/ray references and Original/EX model comparisons pass. Both NVIDIA
application matrices pass 30 exact SBS images; all five Intel non-water cases
pass 25 exact captures. Intel reflected water remains an open gate: even an
unchanged baseline repeat is not identical to itself. All 72 quiet old/new
1x/2x timings finish; repeated Venom medians improve, but some tails worsen and
long-frame stalls persist. This is host timing, not broad FPS or release
acceptance. The final arithmetic checker repeats all six adapter bit checks.
Actual K/M/K switching, all 23 Preview-OFF cases and RENDERING loading also
pass on the unchanged CB42012F executable. The targeted hidden-window trace
localizes its spikes to presentation rather than scene encoding; visible
counterparts lack those spikes, and disabling the SDK does not remove them
from hidden Corneria. This is not proof of a gameplay/SDK stall or broad
visible performance. The separate 72-process visible quiet matrix finishes:
sampled SBS median work falls 7.67–27.06%, with slightly worse D3D12 2x p95.
It does not reproduce the half-second hidden spikes; all raw trials remain
recorded separately. These are two-scene host timings, not displayed FPS or
all-device/release acceptance.
The release and settings are unchanged. See RENDERER-PERFORMANCE-OCT1.md for
exact evidence, failed checks and the unchanged full-goal scope.

October 2 renderer follow-up: current diagnostic `CDDE775F...` fixes shared
CPU row-table mutation in the opt-in parallel stereo encoder. Actual NVIDIA
D3D12/Vulkan and Intel Vulkan ownership, queued-underlay and mixed-layer
stress references pass; both dedicated ray geometry suites pass. Final
D3D12/Vulkan suites each pass 30 exact full-SBS captures, actual DLSS switching
passes, and all 23 Preview-OFF cases plus RENDERING loading pass. The older
72 timing records belong to the superseded `14EA53D3...` candidate, not this
fixed executable. Parallel remains off by default; release `828D98F4...`
and saved settings remain unchanged. Sustained GPU/SBS performance, physical
Leia/reconnect, Android-device relaunch and enhancement parity remain open;
Ally troubleshooting is user-deferred. See GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md and
RENDERER-PERFORMANCE-OCT1.md for exact evidence and limits.

September 24 destruction follow-up: desktop and PCVR now interpolate retained
explosion-face progress between source frames without changing integer source
ticks. Original/EX fractional GPU-vs-Software `KICHI_0` checks pass 90 images
each; targeted desktop and VR tests pass. Newly spawned debris and full natural
death sequences still need visual/device coverage. See PRESENTATION-PARITY.md.

September 23 Intel GPU follow-up: a compact Intel-only DXIL clipping shader
avoids the prior forced D3D12 startup crash. An exact-binary64 GPU billboard
shader fixes the subsequent one-pixel sprite offset. All 59 numbered entries
and five special routes now pass strict sampled Intel D3D12 native/final
Software parity with zero CPU-image uploads; targeted Intel Vulkan and NVIDIA
D3D12 checks pass too. All 59 entries also pass forced Intel Vulkan. The
measured Intel D3D12 default is verified by Original/EX cold-start captures;
the ordinary Android arm64 Debug APK rebuilds and passes its payload check.
Physical-device performance and iPhone 4× acceptance remain open. See
GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md.

September 23 compositor follow-up: color and geometry metadata now share one
native sample calculation per output pixel. Vulkan/D3D12 compositor suites and
targeted 4× Intel Vulkan/1× D3D12 live parity checks pass; a single noisy
hidden-window comparison does not prove sustained FPS or resolve iPhone 4×
exits. See GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md.

September 23 GPU special-route follow-up: all five defined Original/EX
special-route entries also pass 60-frame strict zero-CPU-image-upload parity
checks on Vulkan and D3D12. At this checkpoint, one-thread and alternate
DXIL optimization diagnostics had not resolved forced Intel D3D12; the newer
Intel entry above supersedes that status. See GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md for
scope and evidence.

September 23 strict GPU stage sweep: all 59 numbered Original/EX entries pass
on both D3D12 and Vulkan at 1×/16:9, eight frames after 1,000 source ticks.
Each backend's 472 GPU frames has exact Software native/final parity, zero
CPU-image upload, and no scene readback/replay. This is sampled stage-entry
coverage, not every route frame or a physical-device FPS result; see
GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md.

September 23 Controls GPU migration: its high-priority foreground now starts
inside the ordered late GPU layer, removing a 465,920-byte first-frame CPU
image upload. Original/EX 1×, EX 4× Vulkan and EX 1× D3D12 sampled captures
match Software exactly, with zero upload/readback in 120 GPU frames each. See
GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md; device FPS is not established.

September 23 EX intro GPU migration: an early portrait/dialogue caused 14 CPU
image transfers (490,640 bytes) in 120 frames. Routing host ink through the
ordered late GPU layer removes them. Original/EX 1× and EX 4× sampled intro
captures match Software exactly; the strict audit reports zero CPU-image
uploads/readback on every GPU frame. This is not a full intro or physical-device
performance claim; see GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md.

September 23 GPU ending migration: the two constant inner cartridge border
strips now use compositor constants, eliminating their first-frame full CPU
image upload. Original boss-roll visible/closed captures match Software
byte-for-byte at 1×, with zero CPU-image upload/readback over 451 GPU frames;
its 4× opening frame also matches exactly with zero upload. See
GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md for scope and evidence. This is not all-scene or
physical-device acceptance.

September 23 resident GPU palette transfer: the compositor now skips the
staging map/copy pass when both CPU image and palette are unchanged. The 4×
pre-game menu uploaded one palette in 90 frames; a live EX 6-1 fade uploaded
two in 60 frames, with exact GPU/Software output in both fixtures. Vulkan and
D3D12 compositor suites pass. This reduces transfer work; no FPS or physical
iPhone/Android stability improvement has been established.

September 23 iOS physical startup: a sideloaded 0.0.6.7 package launched on
one iPhone after `Starfox-Assets.BIN` was supplied through iTunes File Sharing.
The published SDL file picker is unsupported on iOS; current source adds a
UIKit picker with main-thread dispatch, but it has not yet been built or tested
on the device. Two app exits occurred with 4× upscale (menu and gameplay),
while a brief 1× run did not exit. No matching crash report was available, so
the 4× fault remains open. See GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md for the desktop GPU
parity/performance evidence, which does not establish iPhone stability.

September 23 GPU transition audit: Original and EX Game Over and Original
BOOT match Software at 4× for 90 sampled frames each, with zero CPU-image
uploads throughout. The compositor metadata lookup now shares one source
projection; Vulkan/D3D12 compositor cases and 4× EX gameplay parity pass.
Intel integrated 4× performance is still poor and no consistent gain is
claimed. The ordinary Android arm64 Debug APK rebuilt and its package check
passed, but device-side GPU behavior is untested.

September 23 ordinary Android GPU startup mitigation: [issue #64](https://github.com/kandowontu/starfox-enhanced/issues/64)
reports a GPU-only freeze when entering preview or play; its reporter says
Software works. New/upgraded installs now select Software once, while GPU
remains an explicit option. A persistent GPU-in-progress marker recovers the
next launch to Software after a hang during renderer creation or the first
120 scene frames, re-arming on menu/scene transitions. Android `startup.log`
is now written beside `pregame.cfg` in app storage for device diagnosis.
The ordinary arm64 APK rebuild and package-content check pass. This is a
startup escape hatch, not a demonstrated Vulkan driver fix: no ordinary
Android handset is attached, and issue #64 remains open pending logcat and
physical retest. A GPU failure after the guarded transition window may still
require manually selecting Software.

contain the actual runtime captures, commands, limitations and historical
checkpoints. Newer user instructions take precedence over the original goal:
VR/Steam physical validation is deferred, Software may keep Enhanced Shadows,
and the upgraded-laser label is Beam.

## Additional user-requested work still open

- Enhanced Sky includes Corneria/Original Training, Macbeth, Titania, Venom,
  Fortuna clouds and several EX landscapes/orbital scenes. Full coverage,
  unique moon upgrades and remaining EX menu mappings are unfinished.
- Enhanced terrain has geometry and targeted captures, not all-stage visual or
  sustained-performance acceptance. Ray secondary-hit fidelity and the horizon
  blend still need broader review.
- Both SDL effects and DXR now cache unchanged backdrop image uploads;
  sustained all-scene performance still requires wider measurement.
- Hardware-dependent acceptance must not be inferred from a successful build,
  a mock device, a package inspection or a software reference comparison.

Keep the full goal active until its actual requirements are proven. Do not
close open GitHub issues, publish a release, deploy to a headset or discard
user data merely to make this index look complete.

October 3 compatibility addition: desktop Windows x64 now has an optional
direct SR Platform output alongside DisplayXR. See [SR-PLATFORM.md](SR-PLATFORM.md)
for setup, exact local artifacts and scoped checks. Missing-runtime fallback,
owned BGRA texture handling and Preview-OFF behavior pass locally; physical SR
panel/lens acceptance and broader native enhancement parity remain open.
This addition does not complete the full goal or update the published release.

October 3 native liquid/TAA follow-up: reconstruction-first paths now defer
packet palette styles in ray materials and environment faces as well as the
primary raster. This prevents optical liquid radiance from receiving a style
before reconstruction and again in the ordered centre-grid post pass. The
independent full-image fixture now draws the backdrop before actors, matching
game assembly; its required wet/dry/protected coverage is retained, not waived.
D3D12 and Vulkan each pass 108 cases over four colour formats and three TAA
qualities, including banked/moving water and eyes, held frames, rejected-frame
recovery and nonblocking cancellation. Evidence:
`D:/SFE-validation/taa-liquid-post-raw-rays-actors-oct3/results.json`.
This is post-order/ownership validation against independent unstyled timelines,
not an independent optical water/depth-finish oracle, true secondary motion,
physical Leia-panel acceptance or a performance claim. The full goal stays open.
